ParlaMint-AT_2014-12-11-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00055_d7e4466	First of all, let me tell you: The Liechtenstein Foundation Law and the Austrian Foundation Law are not identical. The Liechtenstein Foundation law provides for extensive influence by the founder and provides for a mandate contract. Two points should be mentioned in relation to this situation. First: The discretional foundations are covered by the tax agreement. It is therefore not correct that such discretionary trusts are not covered by the tax agreement with the Principality of Liechtenstein. Instead, an appropriate solution has been found for these structures, both for the legalisation of the past and for future taxation, in the framework of the tax agreement. Secondly, I had a conversation with the head of government in Liechtenstein. We have agreed that now at the technical level, it will be examined whether everything that has been agreed is also delivered – especially as regards the exchange of data. Since there is also the instrument of group requests in the future, we will also use this instrument in addition. From Liechtenstein, it has been promised that one is ready to cooperate and that there will be some kind of evaluation of the state of the art in relation to this tax agreement. It is true that the estimates that were higher will not occur in this year.

ParlaMint-AT_2014-12-11-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00055_d7e8825	Mr President. Mr. Minister! High house! Values Advisors! The duration of the mandate of the external auditor of the annual accounts of the Austrian National Bank has so far been limited to one year. By the revision of § 37 para. 1 of the National Bank Act should now extend the term of office to up to five years. The extension is justified, inter alia, by the possibility for auditors to devote themselves to an audit subject for a longer period of time. We reject such extension and advocate a maximum of three years' term of office, for the following reasons: If the annual accounts of the Oesterreichische National Bank are reviewed for a period of five years by the same auditor or audit firm, the examiner will easily experience operational blindness. In addition, too long a term of office between the auditor and the management of the audited entity may create a special relationship of trust which is detrimental to the critical distance and objectivity of the auditor. The auditor's concessions to the audited company with regard to the audit judgment are less likely for a shorter term of office, as the auditor will be replaced after no later than three years. This would also improve the quality of the audit. In the economy one is much more sensitive with regard to the tester rotation, without being legally obliged to do so. For example, the listed Wienerberg Group writes the audit every three to four years. I would therefore like to make the following request:

I would therefore like to pick up a little on the first point, on the secondary market return. This is the law concerning the determination of the average yield for federal bonds in circulation weighted. It should be noted here that the secondary market return or, in fact, several secondary market returns are very, very important indicators for the calculation of interest rates which de facto each Austrians and Austrians are included in their credit contracts, but they are equally important to calculate the premiums for, for example, the construction-saving business, i.e. the construction-saving premium. All these indicators have so far been calculated by the OeKB. Due to a change in the regulatory environment, the OeKB, the Oesterreichische Control Bank, can do so with 1. Do the last time in April 2015. It is therefore a matter of determining and defining conversion factors. This is done in the current law, and in the parliamentary process I mentioned earlier, we have succeeded in defining not only one but four different conversion factors. Why four? – Because in the credit contracts we all actually had different SMRs, i.e. secondary market returns that I mentioned earlier, there were therefore four conversion factors. This gives our citizens the highest degree of legal certainty and clarity for all Austrians. The second issue is the Insurance Supervision Act. In line with the EU Directive of 2009, the so-called Solvency II, the Solvency Directive II, the implementation of a new supervisory system for local insurance is also carried out in an international way, but here it is for local insurance companies. This provides for a fundamental realignment of own resources requirements as well as for further measures in the field of supervisory law of the FMA. In detail maybe four points, so you can imagine something: This is about risk-optimised own resources, so that the safety of the company, which is very important for consumers, is also about the establishment of a governance system, i.e. an internal supervisory system that corresponds to international developments. Thirdly, the adjustment of the supervisory instruments of financial market supervision and also a transparent report on solvency, i.e. payment capacity, towards the public, and much more. However, I would also like to add at this point that we must not raise an overarching regulatory rage and that we must not let it up to the insurance companies, because in the end this is about expenditure and costs. That means the golden middle road is also in demand. Allow therefore It may also be a sentence to the next item on the agenda, which fits precisely here, to the Banking Rehabilitation and Resolution Act. We are thus implementing the second pillar of the Banking Union in Austria. You know, at European level, this bank settlement and restructuring fund is coming. In the future, these funds have all banks in Europe to ensure that taxpayers are not burdened – a very good approach, very important. At the same time, however, it should be noted that in Austria too we do not need overregulation or double taxation. You know, we already have a kind of bank levy that needs to be credited for this – because, as I said, we do not need overregulation, not double taxation, but, quite the contrary, reasonable legal framework conditions, which also strengthen the competitiveness of local banks and insurance companies and thus the whole Austrian economy. Thank you.

It is positive in this context that growth in Austria was still better than in the euro area, that the increase in self-employed workers was higher, the current account balance was better, the exports of goods were higher than in 2012. The deficit has declined if the result budget is to be recovered. At the same time, however, it is also necessary to look at the second side of the medal, which shows that unemployment has increased significantly, from 7 percent to 7.6 percent after AMS calculation, and thus, in 2013 there was more unemployment by 26,523 people than in 2012. The debt ratio has also increased, only the adjusted financial debt of the federal government has increased from €189 billion to €193 billion and, finally, after new ESA calculation, total debt has also increased by €2.7 billion to €262 billion. This also shows that there is a need for action. Austria is not isolated, Austria is in this case in the European Semester, in the Fiscal Pact and other regulations. It should also be noted that, as early as July 2013, Austria should have a structurally balanced budget in 2015 and for itself. The strategy report on the Federal Financial Framework Act shows that this has not been fulfilled. This led to that on 5 May. In May 2014, it was pointed out that Austria is at risk of significantly deviating from the Stability and Growth Pact. This led to the end of the 12th century. In May 2014, the Ministry of Finance reported further measures of €980 million, with the result that the European Commission has delivered an opinion on this, namely that there is still a risk of a significant deviation from the Stability and Growth Pact and, at the same time, that – which has been mentioned over many years – in Austria there would be a need to clean up competence among the local authorities. This, in turn, has led to the 2015 budget overview, which was just discussed, showing that this structurally balanced budget is not achieved in 2015 and that there is a deviation of around €1.5 billion. This also led to the Minister of Finance on 27 July. On 1 October 2014 another 1 billion € and 35 million € have to be reported on improvements. .......................................................................... This shows, therefore, that we are not isolated, and 2013 shows – and this is what the European Commission thinks – that we need measures. This means that structural measures are necessary. It also concerns medium-term planning. In 2013, €75.6 billion was pre-issued, and in 2018 we will have €80.5 billion in pre-expenditure, i.e. we will use €4.9 billion more in funds, 84 percent of which we need for increased pensions. In the future, they will only increase by 0.3 percent. If you look at the breakdown of work, then you see that between 2009 and 2013 the expenditure for work per year has averaged 3.2 per cent, in the years 2013 to 2018 they should average a third less, namely only more than 2.3 per cent. Unemployment will increase. According to Wifo, we have received an average unemployment rate of 8.1 percent in 2015, now unemployment is 8.8 percent. There will be a question of how one third less expenditure on work can actually be found. That is to say, it is positive that a structurally balanced budget is sought, it is positive that a tax reform is also intended to strengthen purchasing power, but at the same time it is clear that without a coherent overall financial approach with structural reforms, we cannot achieve the objective we need in order to be internationally competitive. For this reason, no matter what action is put in place, no matter what position prevails, it is necessary to put on the legs exactly this overall financial concept, in which both the deposit side and the payout side are considered together and under the line a balance stands that goes in the direction that inter-periodal generation justice is maintained. At present, as was the case in 2013, our generation is living at the expense of the future and resource consumption is much higher than we actually do. Finally, I would like to thank you once again for the cooperation. I should like to point out once again: The figures and facts show that we need reforms. The Court of Auditors' reports also indicate the direction in which it could go. Thank you.

I think it is necessary for us to talk briefly about what this change of law is all about. By applying the Media Cooperation and Promotion Transparency Act, our national tourism organisation, the Austrian advertising company, has an administrative burden of more than €70 million – which is also a damage – by stating which media cooperation it has received abroad to apply for the tourist location Austria. The second disadvantage from the application of this law is that every other foreign national tourism organisation can now take a closer look at how the Austrians, who are known to be the world champions of tourism and who have developed a really strong position, can then simply and cheaply cut off from us. That's not the point of the matter. This is damaging our own interests, and I am therefore also a co-applicant of this amendment. But I'm also saying: This change of law is certainly not the final conclusion of wisdom, because I honestly see no reason why it is only the foreign tourism marketing organisations or the cooperations abroad. It's the same domestically: Our tourism organisations are in competition, national and international competition, and why, for example, should the Arlberg tourism organisations be able to look at how and what the Kitzbühelers are doing? This is an blatant distortion of competition, but not just on this issue. Another issue is particularly important to me because I am also a Supervisory Board in a national company and I know the problem myself. There are a lot of companies owned by the public sector, for example in my case, a thermal spa in Upper Austria, 99 percent owned by the state, 1 percent owned by the public sector. These do not have any sovereign duties, in national and international competition, are profit-oriented, have a private business subject, belong to the taxpayer. We should therefore support them in obtaining as much as possible for taxpayers. But by this law we are forcing them – Mr Auer, let me speak out. [[Abg. Auer: They also get state support!]] They also get state subsidies. With this law, we force them to disclose. This enables the 20 kilometres away private-economically oriented competitor to see exactly how the market leader in Upper Austria does – and can do so. This will damage our own interests and, above all, the interests of the taxpayer. In conclusion: I am therefore of the opinion that this change that we are deciding today is good, is all right, but can only be a first step in the repair of this law that is really broken in large areas.

I have been a cultural speaker for almost a year now, and of course you meet as a cultural speaker with many representatives, representatives of art and culture, with people working in the cultural field, and those who produce art. Especially in recent weeks, I have been at many panel discussions on the social situation of artists. Yes, of course, there are very many precarious employment conditions in this area. People often have to switch between self-employment and an employee relationship. Especially for actors, photographers and camera people, this means major problems in terms of billing times, insurance years, social insurance and unemployment insurance. But also visual artists, especially young artists, who are invited to exhibitions, often cannot afford the material costs for the exhibitions at all. I am convinced that politics must create the framework conditions in order to enable art and culture in Austria and above all to allow artists to live on it. A number of things have happened in this area last year. For example, we have legislated the film and television agreement, we have introduced a price link for e-books since 1. December is in force, we've made a novel of film promotion. Many good things have already been done and there is also a lot in this area. For example, there is a gallery promotion, there is the purchase of young art, artotheque, photo collection. There are 95 start scholarships for young artists each year with 6 600 € per person. There are a lot of project funding, which specifically looks at the fact that young people are also present; and of course there is also appreciation and recognition, so many awards in these areas. That's why I'm glad it finally worked out. We have talked and negotiated for a very long time, but I am proud of what we hope will be decided here today. What's new? – The definition of the artist concept becomes contemporary, it will be extended. In the future, the artists have the choice of whether only income – that was so far – or even revenue – is new – is used to achieve the claim. The lower limit is now easier to reach. I admit that I would have liked to abolish the lower limit, but that was not possible. The extension of the ceiling from the 60-fold to the 65-fold of the ASVG basis is new. The revenue from independent artistic secondary activities, i.e. mediation and teaching, can now be taken into account. New is the calculation time, so you have longer time. One point I would particularly like to highlight is the support fund, which was set up, with a sum of €500 000. Unfortunately, there are always artists who may not be able to afford the rent or the electricity, and then it will be a little short. This support fund should be able to help in these cases without problems, and I am glad and proud that it has also been set up.

ParlaMint-AT_2014-12-11-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00055_d7e20460	Mr President. Mr. Minister! High house! We all enjoy the cultural events, the countless activities that take place in our country, both in the city and in the countryside. Austria is a country of culture, with a huge number of creative employees in the cultural institutions, most of them volunteer, but most of them also in precarious work situations. As pleased as this cultural offer is, the current situation is regrettable for many cultural workers in the world of work. Our social network builds on permanent working conditions. That is, unemployment insurance, health insurance, pension insurance presupposes that there are permanent jobs. In the creative economy, however, these permanent jobs are only partially available. A very large part of the creative has precarious working conditions or mixes of short-term working relationship and employed and on the other hand independent or new self-employed, and this is a mix of them. In our social systems, this mix does not allow them to be equally insured as people who are employed and have an annual employment relationship or at least a seasonal employment relationship in which they are socially insured. This gap in our system was then closed or tried to clean it up, namely with the artist social insurance. As Mr Zinggl has said, this has always been somewhat improved. However, I believe that today's improvement is not a small step, but a very, very big one. Now there is the change that income is not used – i.e. revenue less costs, and then we see whether man has income of a commercial nature – but that we are based on revenue alone. This new law will entitle a large number of artists to come to grants. In principle, the basis of assessment has been increased at the income limit. The subsidy limit was increased from €1,026 to €1,722. At the same time, we have set up a fund to help in emergencies. If, because of the precarious situation, the requirements cannot be met, we should nevertheless be able to help. But I believe that we need to think creatively about how we deal with people in situations that no longer have their working environment in permanent jobs. This concerns not only the creative economy, but also very many EPCs, in the IT sector et cetera, where this is very common: short-term employment, but in the meantime, independent activity. We do not yet have a satisfactory answer to this question. It's up to us to work on it.

ParlaMint-AT_2014-12-11-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00055_d7e21067	Mr President. Minister! My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! The signing of the film agreement between Austria and Israel is a very encouraging event based on strong economic and artistic interests. But it certainly also has a political dimension, an added value in the field of international understanding. In this agreement, there is nothing less than the fact that two continuously emerging film nations receive an additional industry impulse through bilateral facilitations. In addition to facilitating production, the Film Agreement also allows the granting of mutual exploitation promotions. This allows a purely Austrian film to facilitate access to non-Austrian markets and, vice versa, to promote the dissemination of non-Austrian quality films in Austria. Movies from Community productions are also made equal and are also promoted equally. The Austrian film industry consisted of more than 2,200 companies in 2012 and had over 7 000 employees. So this is an emerging and important sector. 53 films have been first performed and a strong demand impulse from the film industry. There was an increase in gross investment of more than 23 percent in the previous year. The agreement takes effect at a time when a emerging film industry is matured in both countries. Cooperation between countries cannot be based on continuity, but there is and has always been cooperation. The critical documentary “Putins Spiele” about the Winter Olympics in Sochi is an Austrian-Israeli-German co-production, which also received the German Television Award. Next year, the elaborate feature film “Bumschlager” will be released on the market, which has been created across the state. The marketing and publication contributes safely to the artistic and economic fertilization of the film industry of both countries. It certainly also promotes the internationalisation of outstanding talents in a different, more peaceful spirit than the director Billy Wilder had to experience through expulsion from Austria. He has also shown, however, the high-level flight of Jewish jokes and the youth in Vienna. We all know Billy Wilder got seven Oscars in Hollywood. Thank you.

"The prisons are built out of the stones of the laws", William Blake, an English poet from 18th century BC. ==References== The will of the law for the execution is clear: It's about prevention and victim protection. Society and the victim are to be protected from new crimes. It must be prevented that the specific offender becomes guilty again, and that is what we call special prevention; it is precisely to support the fact that a perpetrator does not commit any new crime. In addition, a punishment should in principle deter people from taking action, which we call preventive effect or general preventive effect. For these reasons, there are fines and imprisonments in Austria. Punishment and execution must therefore be! The new organisational unit will have all the competences, namely planning, organisation, management, management, legal protection and public relations. In the future, people who are affected by violence – and above all women, i.e. citizens – will be better protected abroad. We have already debated the implementation of the protection regulations here, in the context of the National Action Plan, which we discussed here during the last plenary session. Measures to protect victims of criminal acts directed against them, such as bans on entry, contact and proximity, have been in the Austrian law on the protection of violence for more than ten years. These will continue to have effect in other EU Member States than in those in which they were first adopted. And that means specifically to the victims: A measure arranged in Austria to protect a person from violence can be continued in the territory of another EU country, through a measure appropriate to its own national law. The person concerned lacks the need for a separate procedure. Instead, the Austrian decision shall be notified and implemented by the competent authority. Protection measures arranged in criminal proceedings shall therefore be recognised by the State of residence and residence of the protected person. The protection therefore accompanies the potential victim who moves his residence or residence to another Member State. This improvement in judicial cooperation is consistently positive, because, on the one hand, protection of violence is Europeanised, on the other, we are addressing the increasing mobility of people. In this way we increase the safety of women across borders. The legal protection of the victim in the event of family violence thus becomes international, that is right and good so. ParlaMint-AT_2014-12-11-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00055_d7e23695	Mr President. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen. Dear entrepreneurs – because you are mainly concerned with this law, and everyone who likes to read and be allowed to read balance sheets. In 1990, the Accounting Act was first adopted by the EU and fundamentally amended. I was still a student at the time and then a career candidate and was allowed to work actively in my company during the first introduction of this law. It was very laborious at the time, because the implementation of the directive was such that everything stood inside, but one had to look very carefully: Where to find the right section? What is applicable to my company? For what scale does it fit in the final effect? A new regulation is now being introduced with this Accounting Amendment Act. Although it is the implementation of a directive, it has been implemented in such a way that it is well understood. It is not a flick, and it has very positive aspects. On the one hand, there are, and has already been, differences between small, medium and large enterprises and, of course, now. The micro-enterprises are coming up again. What is special about this law and what I would like to emphasize specifically is the legibility of the law. It is written as it corresponds to the motto “Think Small First”. That is, the small entrepreneur starts to read at the beginning and reads his paragraphs that are applicable to him. If his company grows, he must read on and apply the later paragraphs. What is very, very positive – and where I can only contradict Mr Vetter – is: It is a massive administrative simplification especially for micro-enterprises. Here, a micro-regime is implemented, where capital companies with up to €700 in turnover, €350 000 in balance sheet amount and 10 employees are considered micro-enterprises. These micro-enterprises have huge advantages: You don't need to make an annex anymore. They thus save themselves substantial production costs of the annual accounts and thus also costs of tax advisors, costs in their own offices. The reduction of the penalty is also very welcome, because it makes a difference whether a large group does not comply with its publishing obligations or the small baker on site, where the general interest is likely to end. There is also a relief for normal small GmbHs, where the annex is also reduced by 50 percent. There is also a modernisation of tax law. The Austrian specifications fall away like un taxed reserves, which have not been understood internationally. New valuation principles, new depreciation times, are included. The thresholds have also been increased accordingly. A goal of this law is the harmonisation of company law and tax law. A principle of reliable estimation will be introduced and new definitions will be made. The expenditure provisions are defined, interest rates, latent taxes. And even the credits, if once an extraordinary depreciation was, are redefined and properly regulated. Ladies and gentlemen, it is up to us here as a National Council, as income tax legislators and corporate tax regulators to continue to implement this harmonisation and to take this part in the framework of the tax reform, because this too is a loss of derogations and a simplification if there are fewer and less invoices. However, this law is also linked to an increase in transparency – through better international comparability, country-related reporting, more forcefulness in the confirmation notes. We also had this topic today: So far, the auditor could only say: Yes, I give the confirmation note! or: I do not grant it!, now the report can carry the corresponding content so that there are statements so that the balance sheet reader can obtain the relevant content. And there are clear rules on consolidation. Ladies and gentlemen, the Accounting Act is probably the dry matter that is not interested in all here. But what can we learn from it? – Even such a dry matter can be implemented in such a way that it is designed for the legal user in such a way that he understands that it is made with an eye that the smaller companies have easier applications and is more transparent in the larger ones. Many wishes, many long-standing wishes of the Chamber of Trustees could be implemented. In the review procedure, the suggestions were taken into account. This is the first time that we have been able to And what's special is: We decide the law today, and we now have a year to implement it. In other words, all farms can prepare in such a way that the 2016 balance sheet applies exactly this law. Mr. Minister, thank you very much for this implementation! Thanks to your employees for this direction decision! I wish that the laws are often explained with this focus and in this direction, that the legal users have the information they need, and that all objections are implemented. - Thank you.

What does that mean? – These are garages, basements, garden shares and so on. For many decades, this accessory has not been entered separately in the land register, but the housing property contract includes this accessory, which was also transferred to residential property at the same time. A decision by the Supreme Court gave rise to uncertainty insofar as the Supreme Court was in favour of the effective justification of housing property. without inclusion in the basic book. The current repair of the housing property law now makes it clear that the registration of the apartment in the ground book automatically covers its accessories as well. This is, of course, a requirement for a corresponding contractual definition. This, in turn, gives legal certainty to many of the housing owners concerned. I am pleased that this clarification has come about. Thus belongs to the owner of the dwelling, what he has bought or received in good faith from the seller. – That on the question of accessories. Secondly, it has also been clarified whether the landlord or the tenant meets the obligation to maintain a shared heating thermal or hot water boiler. The case law of the Supreme Court was also in the past uneven, and this led to uncertainty both the tenant and the landlord. A change in this regard now makes it clear that the landlord is now responsible for maintaining the heating thermals, the hot water boiler and other heat treatment equipment. The tenant continues to be responsible for the maintenance of these equipment. Ladies and gentlemen! With this small residential property novel, there is legal certainty for owners, for landlords and for tenants. However, I am also aware that further reforms must follow in order to increase the right of residence for the population to be fairer, more understandable, more transparent and more manageable. But the big and central question we need to discuss here is the feasibility of living. And if I now listen to the FPÖ and the Stronach team, both supposed representatives of the little man, then the Voodoo economy consists in saying that if we release the rental price if anyone can demand what he wants, then it will be cheaper. [[Abg. Schrangl: Building more apartments!]] This is voodoo-economics, that will not work, and that follows a very simple economic law. If a good is scarce and someone has to consume this goods, because living is a basic need because he cannot choose like a TV or a car, whether he consumes this product, then it will necessarily result in housing becoming more expensive. Now, Mr Schrangl is presenting our housing as a solution. Of course, it needs a mix, it needs new construction, but it also needs rental interest regulation. Why the new building alone cannot work has a simple reason. In Vienna, 20 000 people increase each year. In order for the new construction on the market to lead to a rental price reduction, you need a huge new construction, because this would have to lead to an emptying. Only when there is an empty stand will this result in the rental price falling. That's just impossible. Therefore, this mix needs regulation on the one hand and new construction on the other. One thing I say to you, I am proud that we do not yet have a situation in Vienna, as in London and Paris, that the middle class can no longer afford accommodation within the belt and in certain external districts that are in demand. Even in Munich, 300 kilometres from Vienna, this is so. No Municher will realistically ask for an apartment close to the center. That is simply no longer eligible for funding. And these are states we don't want. I get calls from families who say we earn 2 000 € and get only more one apartment by 1 000 €. How's that going? And here politics is required. This is not the case with the voodoo economy of the FPÖ and the Stronach team, but only with a concrete mix of measures. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] So, Minister, and now I have to come to you. This is staggered, this rate increases to 63 percent after 10 years of marriage and 66 percent after 20 years of marriage. [[23:11:23]] Conclusions of the President
ParlaMint-AT_2014-12-11-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00055_d7e27507	Ladies and gentlemen! Despite the advanced period of time, I would like to continue with the beautiful tradition and use the end of the last meeting in the calendar year to thank all the employees of the house, the parliamentary clubs and the parliamentary staff for their dedicated and professional work. [[General condolences.]] I would also like to thank personally for the good and constructive cooperation with the Second President of the National Council Karlheinz Kopf and the Third President of the National Council Norbert Hofer as well as with all members of the Presidential Conference. A dark shadow hangs over the end of this year. ==References== August is a great parliamentarian, the President of the National Council Barbara Prammer, died, we had to say goodbye to her, and I know: This is a farewell that has been very difficult for all of us. Ladies and gentlemen! I am not going to list what you have done last year, how many plenary and committee meetings have been held and how many laws have been passed. It was undoubtedly a labour-intensive, busy and lively Parliamentary year, and I think in 2015 many new challenges await us all: We will hold two survey committees, and after today's decision, we can assume that there will be the first investigative committee with this new set of rules. Thank you, ladies and gentlemen, for your work, and I wish you and your families nice, relaxing holidays, and I wish you a good, healthy and successful new year. [[General condolences.]] [[23:14]] The next meeting of the National Council, which will concern business communications and allocations, will be convened at 23:14 a.m., immediately following this meeting. This meeting is closed.
